# Fast engine input bytes against Standard's (2026-11-11)

Every keyboard route and mouse event the Fast engine now encodes with libghostty-vt's encoders, set against what the Java encoders write. Generated by `KeyRoutesDifferencesNoteTest` in terminal-view, which fails when this file is not what the tables generate; regenerate it as that test says.

- **Java** is Standard today, and what the Fast engine wrote before libghostty took over the keys (`debug.tl.terminal_input=java` brings it back). **library** is the Fast engine now.
- Bytes are shown as the program reads them: `\e` is ESC (0x1b), `^X` is the control character Ctrl+X (`^M` is Enter's 0x0d, `^@` is NUL), `\x7f` is DEL, `\xNN` is any byte above 0x7e, `\\`, `\^` and `\|` are a literal backslash, caret and bar.
- Keys are driven through the view's real entry points (hardware key down, repeat and release, extra-keys button, in-app keyboard code point, IME text, raw characters, alternate scroll's `handleKeyCode`); mouse rows call `sendMouseEvent` at column 5, row 3. Terminal state is the kitty flags pushed with `CSI > n u`, DECCKM (cursor keys application) and DECKPAM (keypad application) for keys, and the mouse tracking mode and protocol for the mouse. Rows where both engines write the same bytes are left out.
- A row marked "only Java sends" or "only the library sends" is one side writing nothing.

3108 of 6291 rows differ.
